# Grand Theft Auto — Complete Builds, Versions, Patches, Editions, Ports, Prototypes & DLC Database

> **Status:** ✅ Complete as of 2026-10-24: first research pass, second gap-filling pass and an independent completeness audit, with all audit findings integrated.
> **Compiled:** October 2026 (research date 2026-10-24).
> **Size:** about 2,050 table rows across 15 sections, citing about 930 numbered sources. Confidence markers: about 1,050 ✅, 700 ⚠️, 155 ❓. Fields that were searched for but not found in any source say "not documented", about 400 times.
> **Scope:** Every documented Grand Theft Auto game, from the first *Race'n'Chase* concept (DMA Design, 1995) through *Grand Theft Auto VI*: retail versions, regional/country releases, disc/cartridge serials, executable versions, title updates and patches, re-releases, compilations and special editions, mobile ports, DLC and online updates, demos, press/review builds, leaked/preserved prototypes, and lost or cancelled projects.

## How to read this document

- **Every entry is sourced.** Each section ends with a numbered source list; bracketed numbers such as `[3]` point to it.
- **No guessing.** Where sources conflict, both values are given and the conflict is flagged. Where a build or version is known to exist but its exact number/date was not found in any source, the field reads **"not documented"** rather than an estimate.
- **Confidence markers:**
  - ✅ = confirmed by a primary source (publisher/developer patch notes, platform databases, disc dumps, official press) or multiple independent secondary sources.
  - ⚠️ = reported by a single secondary/community source, or details conflict between sources.
  - ❓ = rumoured/claimed but unverified; included only so it is not "missing", clearly labelled.
- **Dates** use ISO format (YYYY-MM-DD) where the day is known; otherwise YYYY-MM or YYYY.
- **Research limits (read this):** "not documented" means the item was searched for and no source was found. It does not mean the item doesn't exist. During research these sites were wholly or partly unreachable: gtamods.com (offline for maintenance), TCRF, MobyGames game pages, SteamDB, SerialStation, psdevwiki, GTAForums (bot checks), Schnittberichte (server error), and the Wayback Machine (one approved page load at a time). The largest remaining gaps are PC executable sizes and timestamps (gtamods), individual Xbox 360 / PS3 title-update dates, Android per-version dates for the classic mobile ports, release dates for most PC hotfix builds of GTA V, and some German cut reports. Each section ends with its own list of known gaps.
- **Inferences:** where an entry says "inferred", the reasoning is shown and the entry carries ❓ or ⚠️. Inferences are never presented as facts.
- **Regions:** NA = North America, EU/PAL = Europe & PAL territories, JP = Japan, KR = South Korea, AU = Australia, DE = Germany, BR = Brazil, RU = Russia, etc.

## Table of contents

1. Origins & Grand Theft Auto (1997) — *Race'n'Chase* prototypes, GTA 1, London 1969 / London 1961
2. Grand Theft Auto 2 (1999)
3. Grand Theft Auto III (2001)
4. Grand Theft Auto: Vice City (2002)
5. Grand Theft Auto: San Andreas (2004)
6. Handheld & spin-off era — GTA Advance, Liberty City Stories, Vice City Stories, Chinatown Wars
7. Grand Theft Auto IV & Episodes from Liberty City (2008–2010)
8. Grand Theft Auto V — platforms, editions, title updates & PC builds (2013–present)
9. GTA Online — every update & DLC (2013–present)
10. Grand Theft Auto: The Trilogy – The Definitive Edition (2021–present) & Netflix versions
11. Grand Theft Auto VI, leaks, cancelled & lost projects
12. Compilations, collections, bundles & special editions (all eras)
13. Country-specific releases, censorship & localisation (all eras)
14. Completeness audit notes
15. Appendix A — Preserved retail disc & cartridge dumps (Redump / No-Intro): every GTA disc/cartridge/digital package with serials, revisions, sizes and checksums

## 1. Origins & Grand Theft Auto (1997)

Legend: ✅ confirmed by ≥2 independent or primary sources · ⚠️ single community source, or sources conflict (conflict stated) · ❓ rumour/unverified. "not documented" = no source found. Disc data marked "Redump" comes from the Redump PlayStation DAT as mirrored in libretro-database (redump.org itself refused automated access). ROM data marked "No-Intro" comes from the No-Intro DAT mirrored the same way.

### 1.1 Pre-release: *Race'n'Chase* (DMA Design, Dundee, 1995–1997)

| Item | Date | Details | Conf. | Src |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Original design document, "Race'n'Chase" (Game Design doc, **Version 1.05**) | dated **22 Mar 1995**; put online by ex-DMA programmer Mike Dailly on **22 Mar 2011** (Flickr set; later hosted at gamedevs.org) | Pitch for a "fun, addictive, and fast multiplayer car racing and crashing game"; present-day setting; three cities with different graphic styles (New York, Venice, Miami); missions incl. a straight race across the city, a free-roaming demolition derby, a bank robbery; pedestrians can be run over; car theft sets off alarms that bring police; police can leave cars and shoot; cars, maybe boats, helicopters, lorries. Planned platforms: MS-DOS, Windows 95, PlayStation, Sega Saturn, Nintendo 64. Toolchain per Wikipedia (from the doc): Visual C++ 2.0, Watcom C/C++ 10, MASM 6.1, DOS4GW 1.97. GTA.wiki shows 12 page images (cover, revision list, ToC, pp. 4–12). "1.05" is the document's revision, **not** a game build | ✅ (doc, dates, v1.05) / ⚠️ (toolchain) | [1][2][3][4][6] |

| Concept change | 1995–96 (exact date not documented) | Playing as a police officer tested as dull. A bug that made police try to run the player off the road went down well, so the game became about playing a criminal. Title changed from *Race'n'Chase* to *Grand Theft Auto*. Gary Penn later called the early version "awful" and said it was nearly cancelled | ✅ | [1][3][7] |
| Status in an early-1997 interview | early 1997 | Keith Hamilton (director and lead programmer): car handling was being rewritten and graphics were moving to 24-bit | ⚠️ | [1] |
| **"GTA ProtoType 1"** (Mike Dailly) | build **11 Nov 1994** ("11/11/94 was the date on the CD", per Dailly's readme) | Released executable (PC, DOS). Side-on / rotating-grid "cube array" engine written by Dailly in Pascal + assembler on a 486DX66; controls Z/X rotate, < > tilt, Space removes roofs. Readme: the game was "going to be GTA (or 'race and chase' as it was then – cops and robbers was the other name considered)"; dropped because *Syndicate Wars* looked too similar and Keith Hamilton's C rewrite was too slow. Once hosted on mikedailly.com (Wayback 30 May 2008); now on Hidden Palace and BetaArchive. **Note:** this date is earlier than the 22 Mar 1995 design doc and the 4 Apr 1995 development start above | ✅ (HP page + readme, mirrored from Dailly's own site) | [40] |
| **"GTA ProtoType 2"** (Mike Dailly), exe `DINO.EXE` | build **2 Feb 1995** (readme "(2/2/95)") | Released executable (PC, DOS); DINO.EXE contains debug symbols. Top-down proof of concept with zoom, pan, FOV and building-height controls. Readme: named DINO because the code came from an earlier dinosaur prototype, *ATTACK!*, and "DINO.BAT was even shipped" in `\gta\gtados`. After seeing it, Keith, "OZ (David Ozbourn)" and Dave Jones (readme spelling) decided to restart "Race and Chase" on this engine. The readme also says the 24-bit mode (via SciTech UniVBE) "added almost a year to the project", and that the 3Dfx renderer started as a one-week demo for a 3Dfx visit and was then kept for the final release | ✅ (HP page + readme) | [41] |
| Early prototypes (footage) released by Mike Dailly | upload date not documented | TCRF: "two very early prototypes were released by … Mike Dailly" (marked To do). These are the two Hidden Palace builds above. GTA.wiki also has a video section, "Early Grand Theft Auto Prototypes (Footage by Mike Dailly)", and a "prototype map test" image | ✅ | [4][5][40][41] |
| Alternate 320×200×256 graphics set (on retail PC disc) | asset c. 1995 | Uses the old DeluxePaint palette, which points to 1995. GTA.wiki suggests it matches Saturn magazine screenshots (disputed) | ⚠️ | [5][26] |
| Race'n'Chase logo, alternative logo, prototype box art | 1997 (EPK) | Logo from a 1997 Take-Two EPK. Early alternative GTA logo and prototype box art are held on GTA.wiki | ⚠️ | [6][7] |
| **Sega Saturn** port (publisher BMG Interactive) | planned release dates per Sega Retro, from magazines: **Oct 1996** (Intelligent Gamer Aug 1996; Sega Power Aug 1996), **Nov 1996** (Sega FY1997 Brand Review; Ultra Game Players Oct 1996), **13 Dec 1996** (C&VG Jan 1997), **Apr 1997** (Sega Power Feb 1997), **13 Jun 1997** (C&VG Jun 1997), then Dec 1997. Later cancelled; date of cancellation not documented | Covered in Sega Power and Saturn Power #10 previews, plus two early screenshots (csoon.com). Reason: lack of money (GTA.wiki) or "poor sales of the console" (Sega Retro), so sources differ. Keith Hamilton reportedly said the engine was inspired by *Clockwork Knight*, which Dailly's own Prototype 2 readme confirms ("After seeing a Sega Saturn game called 'Clockwork knight', I started toying with side on perspective stuff") [41]. Wikipedia: [citation needed] | ✅ (existence, planned dates) / ⚠️ (reason) | [1][26][41][52] |
| **Nintendo 64** port ("Grand Theft Auto 64") | IGN article 29 Mar 1999 | Cancelled before any public showing | ⚠️ | [1][27] |
| **Palm OS** port (Global Star Software) | announced 26 Mar 2001 (Take-Two PR) for holiday 2001 (EU/NA); reported dead 17 Jun 2002 (Gouranga!) | A demo was released on Global Star's website | ✅ | [1] |

Unused or leftover content on the retail PC disc (TCRF): a helicopter that can't fly (graphics but no name); a tram meant for San Andreas (it kept crashing); a Petrol Bomb item; a Breakdown Truck and an APC with names but no graphics; a debug mode turned on by the player name `porkcharsui`. [5] ⚠️

**Preserved alpha/beta builds:** Hidden Palace (checked Oct 2026) holds the two Dailly engine prototypes above, the PC demos (see 1.2) and one PlayStation pre-release build (see 1.4). It lists no PC alpha/beta of the full game. Hidden Palace gives final (gold) build dates of **EU 16 Oct 1997** and **US 9 Feb 1998** for PC; these match the volume-creation dates of the Redump EU and US discs (see 1.3). TCRF prototype pages could not be read: tcrf.net showed a bot-check page and was not bypassed. [40][41][42][44]

### 1.3 Grand Theft Auto (1997): PC (MS-DOS / Windows 95)

One CD carries both DOS and Windows versions. Windows renderers are chosen in setup: 8-bit software, 24-bit software, or 3Dfx Glide (PCGW: Glide 2.42). DOS uses VESA SVGA. Windows multiplayer uses DirectPlay (TCP/UDP ports 2300–2400 and 47624). Registry key: `HKLM\Software\DMA Design\Grand Theft Auto`. Saves: `GTADATA\PLAYER_A.DAT`. Minimum spec: Pentium 75, 16 MB RAM, 80 MB HDD, 1 MB VESA SVGA. User reports (unverified) name the DOS executables/folders `GTADOS`, `GTA8`, `GTA24`, `GTAFX` and the Windows folder `GTAWIN`. [1][9][37]

### 1.4 Grand Theft Auto: PlayStation (port by Visual Sciences, ViSOS framework)

The PS1 version has no trains, so the missions "Gangsta Bang – Counthash" and "Heist Almighty – Itali" are cut. Only the four male characters are selectable (PC has eight). The Japanese version adds two exclusive characters, **Bison** and **Jack**. [1][7][12][20]

### 1.5 Grand Theft Auto: Game Boy Color (Tarantula Studios; published by Rockstar Games / Take-Two)

Dual-mode cartridge: also runs in black and white on the original Game Boy and on Super Game Boy. 4 MiB (32 Mbit) ROM. Gore and swearing removed; trains replaced by teleports; adds an exclusive character, Kelly; new chiptune soundtrack. ESRB T. [1][7][13][29][39]

GTAboom (30 Jul 2025): the GTA store page was delisted "early last year", i.e. about early 2024. So two stages: sales ended (exact date not documented; before mid-2019), then the store page went (about early 2024). ### 1.7 Grand Theft Auto: Mission Pack #1 – London 1969

Developed by **Rockstar Canada** (later Rockstar Toronto), outsourced by DMA Design. PS1 port: the credits transcribed on GTA.wiki's London 1969 Credits page list "**Conversion by: RuneCraft Limited**" and "**VisOS Programming: Visual Sciences**" (Visual Sciences' VisOS framework, also used for the GTA PS1 port). That explains the old conflict: Wikipedia and GTA.wiki credit Runecraft, PSXDC credits Visual Sciences / DMA, and both companies are in the credits [49]. Originally due to be published by Gathering of Developers; published by Rockstar Games / Take-Two. Announced Feb 1999 (IGN 2 Feb; GameSpot 10 Feb). The PS1 version needs the original GTA disc (disc swap). TCRF: the PC release has separate Windows and DOS graphic sets; the Windows EXE contains "gta2.exe" debug strings (the DOS EXE doesn't); debug mode via the player name `ROMMEL`; unused Petrol Bomb. Topped the UK charts for its first two weeks. [30][32][34][36]

### 1.9 Research gaps (Section 1)

- Resolved in the Oct 2026 pass: the two Dailly prototypes (Nov 1994, Feb 1995) and the PS1 May 1998 beta (Hidden Palace); PC demo build dates and the Japanese trial disc; PC disc versions, serials and master dates (EU 11.5, US 12.0/12.1, AU, JP ZOO-0003, reissues; Redump); PS1 EXE dates, the Platinum pressing and the French demo SLED-01093; London 1969 PC/PS1 disc data, the standalone Japanese PC "Grand Theft Auto: London" and the PAL PS1 beta 37; the London PS1 porter conflict (RuneCraft conversion + Visual Sciences VisOS, per credits); Saturn planned dates.
- PC executable version strings and any official patch: none documented. The only "versions" are Redump disc versions (11.5 / 12.0 / 12.1 / 1.1 / 1.0.0.1). What changed in US 12.1 ("GTARE1") is not documented.
- PC demo build dates: Hidden Palace's own "Aug 29, 1997" vs "Nov 11, 1997" labels conflict. Demo exe names not documented: the Hidden Palace demo pages have no file lists, only the archive names given in 1.2 and Source 42.
- Japanese PC release date: GTA.wiki gives 27 Apr 1999; GameFAQs release data gives 23 Jan 1998 with publisher Zoo Corporation [53]. The 23 Jan 1998 date fits the Dec 1997 disc master and the Mar 1998 trial disc better, but no primary source (publisher announcement, Famitsu listing) was found to settle it.
- Japanese London PC ("Game Fan 1980" edition): release date and publisher not documented.
- Rockstar Classics GTA 1 freeware: no official version number; exact launch date before 7 Mar 2003 not documented (Wayback snapshots of rockstargames.com/classics needed per-load approval and were not taken).
- Steam: exact dates sales ended and the store page was removed (about early 2024) are not documented. SteamDB history is behind a bot check.
- TCRF prototype/prerelease subpages and MobyGames release lists: blocked by bot checks (not bypassed).
- PS1 SLES-03389 London Special Edition: EXE date 2000-11-20 conflicts with PSXDC's 10 Mar 2000 release date; unresolved.
- German-specific (censored) builds: none documented.

## 2. Grand Theft Auto 2 (1999)

Developer: DMA Design (Dundee; lead programmer Keith Hamilton) — GBC port by Tarantula Studios — Publisher: Rockstar Games (Russia PC: Buka Entertainment; Japan PC: Zoo) [1][2][5][6].
Original working title / pre-rebrand name: "Grievous Bodily Harm" (G.B.H.) ⚠️ [4]; rejected working title "Eh Stole E' Motur" [1].

Colour and monochrome demo packages were hosted on palmgamingworld (Wayback 10 Feb 2005) and globalstarsoftware.com/gta_pos (Wayback 1 Dec 2001: colour only, grey "coming soon"; the site claims a 4 MB colour version, but the colour demo is 2.33 MB uncompressed). Port cancelled by Jun 2002 (fan-site sources). Music differs from the GBC GTA 2; title shows "Grand Theft Auto" without the "2". **Note:** Hidden Palace files this under GTA 2, but the announcement text describes the *original* GTA. Which game it is remains ⚠️ (see also Section 1.1) | ⚠️ | [10] |

Demo notes: Take-Two's 21 Oct 1999 shipping press release states the demo had "over 1 million Internet downloads" [13]. Softpedia mirrors the demo as `GTA2DEMO.exe` (12.3 MB) [14]. gtamp.com labels its mirror the "8.6 timed demo" ("playable 6 minute demo") — conflicts with gta.wiki's "v8.9"; exact demo version therefore ⚠️ [3][15].

### 2.2 Original retail releases (1999–2001)

Note: release dates conflict heavily between sources; all variants are listed. Take-Two press release (Business Wire, 21 Oct 1999): PlayStation and PC versions "began shipping to retailers worldwide", >1.2 million units to ship over three days, expected >US$33 m gross revenue in FQ ending 31 Oct 1999 [13].

Dreamcast porting studio: Sega Retro credits the Dreamcast version to "**DMA Design, Liquid Games**" [52]. No second source was found: gta.wiki's Dreamcast page, the GameSpot preview, Grand Theft Wiki ("DMA Design/Tarantula Studios" for all platforms) and The Dreamcast Junkyard name no porter, and MobyGames credits were blocked by a bot check. ⚠️ (single source).

PAL Dreamcast date: the build dates (Redump 2 Jul 2000; Hidden Palace 3 Jul 2000; French disc 16 Jul 2000) and Sega Retro's retailer-sourced dates (UK/DE 4 Aug, FR 2 Aug) all point to **late Jul–Aug 2000**. The 2 May and 20 May 2000 PAL dates are contradicted by every build date [8][48][52].

### 2.3 PC executable versions & patches (official and community)

**Original retail version:** redump.org records **version 9.6** for the original 1999 US and EU retail discs (masters dated 9–15 Oct 1999), and for every later Western reissue it lists (Xplosiv, Gamer's Choice, PC Best Buy, Take Two Heritage, Złota Edycja, Classics Collection 2004). The Russian Buka disc is **10.1** (Nov 1999) and the Japanese Zoo disc **10.5** (Jan 2000) [48]. No dated list of official Rockstar patches was found. Related multiplayer tooling: GTA2 Game Hunter (by Sektor) — first version 24 Jan 2005; v1.483 (13 Feb 2008), v1.484 (15 Feb 2008), v1.485 (22 Feb 2008), v1.487 (6 Mar 2008) [35][36]. Native MP: up to 6 players via DirectPlay (Internet/LAN/serial/modem; ports TCP/UDP 2300–2400, 47624) [2][6].
Debug: registry value `bob_debug_display`=1 under `HKLM\SOFTWARE\DMA Design Ltd\GTA2\Debug` unlocks a Debug tab in GTA2Manager (debug keys, camera/teleport) [7].

### 2.5 Regional / content differences & ratings

- ESRB: PC & Dreamcast M; PlayStation & GBC T (toned-down language/violence) [5][19]. BBFC 18; USK 16; OFLC AU MA15+; OFLC NZ R18; SELL 16 [5]. Japan (PC) R-18 per ja.wikipedia [18].
- Germany: No censored German version documented; game shipped multi-language with German included (PS1 PAL Multi-5) [5][22]. "not documented" for any German-specific cut.
- France (Dreamcast): parental lock code `WFUSDFCF` required at boot, supplied on separate insert ("not appropriate for people under the age of 16"); fr.wikipedia states the code was given out by retailers [27][42]. Redump records the **same code for the French PlayStation disc SLES-02453** (French only, EXE 1999-10-04). The French-specific DC disc is T-40502D-09 (v1.000) [48].
- Germany (disc evidence): no German-only GTA 2 disc appears in Redump. The German Software Pyramide PC reissue and the GameStar cover-disc full versions are multilingual [48].
- Russia: Buka localisation "GTA 2: Беспредел" [16].
- Japan: PC-only Japanese edition via Zoo (30 Mar 2001); DC unreleased in JP [8][17].
- PlayStation vs PC: PS1 day-only, no narrator, fewer cars/peds, 25 tokens per district (PC 50), Residential max wanted 6 (PC 5); PC offers noon/dusk; DC dusk only; GBC day only, password saves, protagonist choice [5][6].
- Blood: ja.wikipedia notes corpse blood removed vs other titles, re-enabled by cheat (region-specificity not stated) [18].

### 2.6 Related media

- **"GTA 2: The Movie"** (unofficial title; film itself unnamed) — ~8-minute live-action short shot in New York City in 1999; written by Dan Houser, directed by Alex De Rakoff; Scott Maslen as Claude Speed. Released on Rockstar's website as 8-part QuickTime or single RealVideo file, and on the Moving Shadow 99.1 mix CD. In-game intro (Bink .bik) cut from the footage at higher quality; step-van driver shooting omitted from game intro [1][6][43]. ja.wikipedia notes a longer version of the intro exists [18].
- GBH soundtrack (pre-rebrand music) published 22 Oct 2024 for 25th anniversary ⚠️ [4].
- Sales: >1.2 m shipped at launch; ~2 million lifetime (Wikipedia; GTA Fandom "over 2 million") [1][6].

### 2.7 Research gaps (Section 2)

- Resolved in the Oct 2026 pass: retail PC disc version (9.6, Redump); Dreamcast serials (T-42102N, T-40502D-18/-61/-09, T-40502D-05 per Sega Retro); GBC cart codes (CGB-BGTP / CGB-BGAE); GTA 2 GBC ROM hashes; PS1 Greatest Hits (same serial, tag SLUS-00789GH); PS1 EU Rev 1 and French SLES-02453; Rockstar Classics date (22 Dec 2004); the Sep 1999 PS1 preview prototype; Patch 1.03 existence (2002 GameStar cover discs).
- Official Rockstar/DMA patch history: still undocumented. Nobody explains how "1.03" relates to exe versions 9.6 / 10.x, or who released it.
- Dreamcast porting studio: only Sega Retro ("Liquid Games"). MobyGames credits (Cloudflare) and TCRF (bot check) could not be read.
- GBC USA cart code CGB-BGAE: gbhwdb only.
- Steam: exact date sales ended (between 2013, an unsourced user claim, and Oct 2022) and exact store-page removal date (mid/late Jul 2025, from news reports). SteamDB history is behind a bot check, and Wayback snapshots needed per-load approval, so they were not used.
- SLES-02458 (psxdatacenter): no Redump match. It may be a typo for SLES-02453 (French) or an undumped disc; not resolved.
- TCRF prototype/prerelease subpages for GTA 2 (Windows): tcrf.net bot check, not read.
- PS1 PAL Rev 1 changes, and the DC French v1.000 vs PAL v1.003 differences: not documented.

## 3. Grand Theft Auto III (2001)

Developer: DMA Design (credited on the original PS2 release), later Rockstar North; publisher Rockstar Games (Capcom for the Japanese-language release; Zoo Corporation for the earlier English-language PC release in Japan). [1] Xbox port by Rockstar Vienna; Mac OS X / iOS / Android / 10th Anniversary ports by War Drum Studios. [1] PCGamingWiki credits TransGaming Inc. as Mac developer. [9] (The 2021 Definitive Edition is covered in its own section.)

Legend: ✅ confirmed by 2+ independent sources or official source · ⚠️ single community source · ❓ rumour / disputed.

### 3.1 Development timeline and pre-release builds

| Version/Build | Platform | Region | Release date | Serial / ID / Exe version | Notes | Conf. | Src |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dreamcast tech demo / early build (cancelled) | Dreamcast | — | c. 1999–2000 (never released) | none | Tech demo built on Dreamcast around end of GTA2 development (1999). After Take-Two bought DMA (Sept 1999) the team started on Dreamcast and moved to PS2 after about four months; Obbe Vermeij: change "not due to hardware constraints", Dreamcast not commercially viable. No build is known to be preserved. | ✅ | [1][4][19] |
| `handling.cfg` file date | (dev files) | — | 10 Dec 1999 | — | Earliest dated asset noted by TCRF. | ⚠️ | [3] |
| Wireframe test #1 (leak) | PS2 dev | — | 21 Jul 2000 | — | Leaked to the Gouranga fansite: protagonist entering/exiting a car. | ⚠️ | [3] |
| Edge renders (Chinatown) | — | UK | June 2000 | — | 3D renders leaked via Edge Magazine (issue not identified). | ⚠️ | [3] |
| Wireframe test #2 (leak) | PS2 dev | — | 9 Oct 2000 | — | Car theft and escape from gang member, leaked to Gouranga. | ⚠️ | [3] |
| Take-Two announcement | PS2 | — | 7 Nov 2000 | — | Next-gen GTA planned for "Summer 2001" on PS2 (archived IGN interview). | ⚠️ | [3] |
| December 2000 build (magazine reveal) | PS2 | UK | Jan 2001 issue | — | Revealed in Official PlayStation 2 Magazine UK (Jan 2001). Mentions dog-walking peds, 30-min day cycle, inter-island ferry, insult button. | ⚠️ | [3] |
| First trailer | PS2 | WW | 19 Feb 2001 | — | Released online. | ⚠️ | [3] |
| E3 2001 build | PS2 | US (LA) | 17–19 May 2001 | — | Shown at E3; HUD nearing final; E3 trailer shows destructible AirTrain and reactive car antennas (later cut). | ⚠️ | [3][19] |
| c. Aug 2001 build (PS World Vol. 12 footage) | PS2 | — | c. Aug 2001 (estimated) | — | Orange logo, bright-yellow weapons HUD, different sniper crosshair, shootable ped limbs. | ⚠️ | [3] |
| Pre-9/11 build (PlayZone Nov 2001 footage) | PS2 | — | 2001 | — | HUD matches final; last appearance of blue police cars and Bobcat with cushioned trunk. | ⚠️ | [3] |
| Delay announcement | PS2 | — | 19 Sep 2001 | — | Release delayed three weeks after 9/11 (originally set for 3 Oct 2001 per GTA Wiki). | ✅ | [1][4] |
| Cancelled GameCube port | GameCube | — | c. 2003 (never released) | none | Planned alongside Vice City; cancelled for unknown reasons. | ⚠️ | [3][19] |

**Documented post-9/11 changes before release** (Rockstar described the game as "about 1% different") [1]:
- Police cars: blue with white stripes (NYPD-like) → black-and-white. ✅ [1][19][21]
- Dodo/plane flight path altered so the aircraft no longer appeared to fly into/behind a skyscraper. ✅ [1]
- A mission referencing terrorists removed ("We removed only one mission that referenced terrorists" — Rockstar Q&A, reported 5 Jan 2012); some pedestrian dialogue, car details and talk-radio content changed. ✅ [1][19][22]
- Original cover art replaced in North America (original art kept in Europe). ✅ [1][4]
- Darkel (revolutionary character) cut — Dan Houser: "months before [release]", i.e. not a 9/11 change. ✅ [1][20]
- School bus / schoolchildren: Rockstar called pre-release schoolchildren reports "nonsense"; GTA Wiki states Darkel never had a school-bus mission (rumour). ❓ [1][19]
- Destructible AirTrain shown in trailers cut. ⚠️ [19]

**Early mission names** (Game On 2009 exhibition, Taiwan): "Lips Forelli's Last Lunch" → "Mike Lips Last Lunch"; "Kill Chunky Lee" → (the Chunky Lee Chong mission); "Escort Town" → "Cipriani's Chauffeur"; "The Bank Job" → "The Getaway"; "Plane to Sea" → "S.A.M."; "Gone in 60 Grand" → "Grand Theft Auto"; "Wanted Man" → "The Exchange". ⚠️ [3]

### 3.2 Preserved / leaked prototype builds

| Version/Build | Platform | Region | Release date | Serial / ID / Exe version | Notes | Conf. | Src |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| "v1.40 PAL Prototype" (Hidden Palace listing) | PS2 | PAL | 26 Oct 2001 (shipped retail) | SLES-50330, disc version v1.40 | Hidden Palace lists the *first PAL retail pressing* as a "very late beta version" released by mistake: ped line "I designed the PS2!", Dodo clipping into building near Portland police station (early flight path), extra Catalina lines in "The Exchange", light-blue police cars in intro, unskippable tutorials, Paramedic 100% quirk. Classing it as a prototype is HP's own claim. | ⚠️ | [18][17] |
| Game Boy Advance port: announcement | GBA | — | E3 2001 | — | At E3 2001 Rockstar announced a deal with Destination Software to bring PS2 games to GBA (Smuggler's Run, Midnight Club, GTA III). Developer: Crawfish Interactive. Cancellation rumours appeared Nov 2002 (denied by Destination). Crawfish then closed about a week later; ex-head Cameron Sheppard blamed "a number of publishers not paying on time". The project went to Digital Eclipse and became the unrelated *Grand Theft Auto Advance* (2004; see its own section). Unseen64 says an even earlier, unnamed GBA version was also reportedly cancelled. | ✅ | [53][54] |
| GBA prototype, 12 Apr 2002 (**new entry**) | GBA | — | build 12 Apr 2002 (never released) | — | Hidden Palace: "very early prototype" of Crawfish's GBA GTA III. **Undumped**; shown in an unlisted PtoPOnline video (PtoPOnline published an early tech-demo video in Dec 2016, per Unseen64). Used for physics testing; camera zoom set manually with the shoulder buttons. | ⚠️ | [53][55] |
| GBA prototype, 16 Apr 2002 (**new entry**) | GBA | — | build 16 Apr 2002 (never released) | — | Hidden Palace: dump status **Released** (released by "portraitoflostsouls"). Same Crawfish GBA project; later build than 12 Apr. Hidden Palace gives no public release date for the file. | ⚠️ | [56] |
| Other leaked GTA III prototype builds | PS2 / PC / Xbox | — | — | — | Hidden Palace's "Grand Theft Auto III prototypes" category holds only the two GBA builds above (the "v1.40 PAL Prototype" page is a retail disc). Its GTA III assets are an Xbox Double Pack video/EPK and a "Rockstar E3 2001 Press Kit". No standalone pre-release PS2/PC/Xbox build (E3/press/preview disc) is publicly preserved in the sources checked (Hidden Palace, TCRF). | — | [2][3][18][57] |

Leftovers in shipped builds (TCRF): unused first-person mode (FPS_PUNCH, FPS_ROCKET anims), scrapped online multiplayer (maps left in mobile port), debug menu, flyable-helicopter code, early "Luigi's Girls" mission script in the iOS port, cut song "Wash Him Off" (Marydancin), internal string "GTA3v2". ⚠️ [2]

### 3.3 PlayStation 2 releases

Note on disc versions: PS2 discs carry a `VER` value in SYSTEM.CNF; Redump names dumps by it (e.g. "(v1.40)", "(v1.60)"). First pass: Redump.org could not be fetched and data came from mirrors. Second pass: the Redump disc pages were read directly in a browser (disc IDs given in the sources), so version, EXE date and edition data below is primary dump-database data. Redump's "Edition" field lists every retail printing that a given disc image was verified against. Matching DAT entries are in Appendix A.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Original release, disc v1.40 | PS2 | NTSC-U (USA, Canada) | 22 Oct 2001 (TCRF, GTA Wiki) / 23 Oct 2001 (Wikipedia, psxdatacenter) | SLUS-20062; disc VER 1.40; EXE date 2001-10-07; CRC 431C2B33, 4,640,997,376 bytes | DVD-5, English only; UPC 710425270796; credited to DMA Design. Redump has only **one** NA disc version, verified against these editions: Original, Greatest Hits, Double Pack, *The Trilogy*, Double Pack Greatest Hits. Volume label GTA3. | ✅ | [1][2][4][13][45] |
| Greatest Hits reprint | PS2 | NTSC-U | not documented (eBay listings label it 2003) | SLUS-20062GH (Redump disc serial); GH cover/disc art SLUS-20062-F-GH / -D-GH | GTA Wiki: price cut to "$19" once it became a Sony Greatest Hits title. Redump: the GH disc is the same v1.40 image as the original (same CRC). Also listed: serial SLUS-20062P3 (a later printing, same image); Double Pack GH spine ID SLUS 27003GH; *The Trilogy* Canadian cover serial SLUS 27006F. | ✅ | [4][13][45] |
| Original release, disc v1.40 | PS2 | PAL (EU) | 26 Oct 2001 | SLES-50330 (also SLES-50330/EURO); disc VER 1.40; EXE date 2001-09-25; CRC B76D10AF | Multi-5 menus (EN/FR/DE/IT/ES), English voices; EAN 5026555300490. Original (pre-9/11-style) European cover. A German or French UI language turns on the censored gore mode (see 3.9). Redump edition: Original only. Hidden Palace treats this pressing as a mis-released late beta (blue intro police cars, "I designed the PS2!" line, etc.). | ✅ | [14][16][17][18][46] |
| Revised disc v1.60 | PS2 | PAL (Europe, Australia) | not documented (EXE date 2001-10-07) | SLES-50330 and variants SLES-50330#, #2, -P, -T, /EURO, /UK, /UK#, /EURO#, -P-GER; disc VER 1.60; CRC 1582E336; DNAS disc ID 00 00 00 00 24; BBFC reg. VFC29060 | Redump editions: Original, **Platinum**, **Double Pack**, so the PAL Platinum and Double Pack discs are v1.60. Redump comment: this version "was banned in Australia shortly after release" and was replaced there by SLES-50793. Reported changes vs v1.40: Dodo flight altitude, Catalina's line in "The Exchange" shortened, intro police cruisers altered, Portland subway gate collision enlarged (last unconfirmed). | ✅ (version/editions) / ⚠️ (change list) | [16][17][47] |
| Platinum reprint | PS2 | PAL | not documented | SLES-50330-P (disc v1.60) | psxdatacenter lists Platinum front cover art. Redump ties the Platinum serial to the v1.60 image. | ✅ | [14][47] |
| Australian censored replacement | PS2 | AU | Jan 2002 (Wikipedia/TCRF date for the edited AU release); psxdatacenter lists 26 Oct 2001 (probably carried over from the original) | **SLES-50793** (also SLES-50793-P Platinum); disc VER 1.00; EXE date 2002-01-17; CRC 11D25D47; EAN 5026555301275 | **Purpose resolved:** Redump says this censored version "was released only in Australia to replace the original release that was banned (SLES-50330)". Manual and map still carry SLES-50330. OFLC MA15+. Redump says "the only difference" is that heads and limbs no longer fly off when shot. That **conflicts** with Wikipedia/TCRF, which say the AU edit removed hiring prostitutes for health. Both claims are kept; this is unresolved. Multi-5 with language selector (psxdatacenter). | ✅ (serial/purpose) / ⚠️ (content difference) | [1][2][15][48] |

Exe reported to correspond to retail 1.1 plus a SteamStub DRM wrapper (⚠️ single blog); PCGamingWiki: most digital versions already include 1.1. A disc check was reportedly left in. German/French censorship removed (TCRF). Vehicle colour bug needing "Steam Car Colour Fix". Steam Cloud saves. Restricted in Germany. Retired from the store (publisher request) on 13 Oct 2021 ahead of the Definitive Edition. | ✅ | [2][9][10][11][12][29][30] |
| Rockstar Games Launcher / Rockstar Store version | Windows | WW | not documented precisely (Delisted Games lists 17 Sep 2019, the date the launcher went live) | not documented | Delisted 11–18 Oct 2021; after the DE launch Rockstar said original PC versions would return to the Rockstar Store as a bundle; DE buyers on RGL (11 Nov 2021 – 30 Jun 2022) were promised the originals free (see compilations section). | ⚠️ | [11] |

Downgrade note: the community "downgraded exe" (1.0-compatible gta3.exe) is widely used because mods (CLEO, Widescreen fix, Windowed mode) target 1.0; the 1.1 patcher does not recognise the Steam exe. ⚠️ [29][30]

### 3.4a PC executable builds recognised by modding tools

Community modding toolkits must tell PC executables apart, so their detection code is evidence of which distinct `.exe` builds exist. Plugin-SDK (the main C++ modding SDK for the 3D-era games) recognises exactly three GTA III executables, identified by the address of a known function prologue (`55 89 E5 53`, read as 0x53E58955). This confirms at least these three distinct builds (v1.0, v1.1, Steam); other executables (e.g. Japanese, Rockstar Games Launcher, Mac) exist but are not handled by Plugin-SDK. It gives no file sizes or dates; the gtamods.com exe table (sizes/timestamps) was offline during research.

| Exe build | Plugin-SDK ID | Detection signature (address → value) | Notes | Conf. | Src |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| v1.0 (English) | GAME_10EN (100) | 0x5C1E70 → 0x53E58955 | Retail v1.0 | ✅ | 65 |
| v1.1 (English) | GAME_11EN (110) | 0x5C2130 → 0x53E58955 | Official patch 1.1 | ✅ | 65 |
| Steam | GAME_STEAM (120) | 0x5C6FD0 → 0x53E58955 | Steam executable; distinct code layout from 1.1 | ✅ | 65 |
| Definitive Edition (Unreal Engine) | GAME_UNREAL | (no address check; separate build target GTA3_UNREAL) | Plugin-SDK supports the 2021 UE4 version as a separate target | ✅ | 65 |

### 3.8 Mobile: *Grand Theft Auto III: 10 Year Anniversary Edition* (War Drum Studios)

Announced by Rockstar in October 2011 (with a 1,500-unit Claude action figure, US$149.99). Described by Wikipedia as almost identical to the Windows version, with enhanced models/textures and touchscreen controls; it was the first 3D-Universe game officially translated into Korean, and the version later sold in South Korea. Contains leftover multiplayer maps, a working debug SCM and mission source files (early "Luigi's Girls"). [1][2][4]

### 3.10 Cross-references

- *Grand Theft Auto: Double Pack* (PS2/Xbox), *Grand Theft Auto: The Trilogy* (2005, PS2/PC/Mac), and the 2021 Rockstar Store classic bundle → compilations section.
- *Grand Theft Auto III – The Definitive Edition* (2021/2023) → Definitive Edition section.
- Fan projects (not official builds): "Grand Theft Auto 3D" pre-release-recreation mod (Jan 2019) [21]; re3 reverse-engineered source (not covered here).

### 3.11 Known gaps

- **Resolved in second pass:** PS2 disc versions (Redump: NA has a single v1.40 disc for all printings incl. Greatest Hits; PAL Platinum/Double Pack = v1.60), the role of SLES-50793 (Australian censored replacement), JP disc versions, Xbox disc serials (TT-014 for all regions), PS3 PSN content IDs, PS4 CUSA IDs/patch, the full iOS version history (1.0 → 1.5) including a separate German iOS app, the regional Android packages (WW/AU/DE/JP) and their current versions, Mac App Store dates, and the GBA prototypes on Hidden Palace.
- **Still open:** gtamods.com was offline ("Back soon", site maintenance) and the Internet Archive was "temporarily offline", so **PC exe sizes/identification data for 1.0 / 1.1 / Steam / Japanese / RGL are still undocumented**. SafeDisc version, PC retail product codes and Mac app version numbers are not documented. Android per-version dates (1.0 → 1.9 / 1.10.x) are not documented: Google Play shows only the current version, APKMirror doesn't carry the classic app, and APKPure, MobyGames, PCGamingWiki, TCRF and SerialStation sit behind Cloudflare checks. EU/AU PS3 release dates come only from Wikipedia. The content difference of the AU edit (SLES-50793) is disputed between Redump and Wikipedia/TCRF. No Korean PS2 or PC release was found (only the mobile version is reported as sold in Korea).
- No publicly preserved standalone pre-release PS2/PC/Xbox GTA III build (E3/press/preview/demo disc) was identified. The only preserved prototype is the cancelled Crawfish GBA port (16 Apr 2002 build on Hidden Palace).

## 4. Grand Theft Auto: Vice City (2002)

Developer: Rockstar North (PS2/PC); Rockstar Vienna (Xbox port, per TCRF); War Drum Studios (2012 mobile port, per TCRF). Publisher: Rockstar Games (Capcom in Japan). Announced at E3, 22 May 2002 [1]. Scope note: the 2021 Definitive Edition and Vice City Stories (2006) are covered in other sections.

### 4.1 Development timeline and pre-release

| Version/Build | Platform | Region | Release date | Serial / ID / Exe version | Notes | Conf. | Src |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Announcement | — | — | 22 May 2002 (E3) | — | Announced at E3 2002. Origin: began as a planned GTA III mission pack, then made stand-alone; full production from late 2001 / early 2002. | ✅ | [1] |
| Release date slip | PS2 | NA | announced 5 Sep 2002 | — | Rockstar moved NA release from 22 Oct to 29 Oct 2002. | ✅ | [1] |
| Oct 1, 2002 prototype (self-labelled "v1.50") | PS2 | PAL (EU) | Compiled 1 Oct 2002 22:34:23 (in-game text shows "Wed 02/10/2002"; some files dated 27 Sep 2002); publicly released 1 Feb 2025 | Disc serial SLES-51061 | Dumped by "johndoe2", released on GTAForums by IAmNotToniCipriani, amplified by Vadim M. (@NationalPepper). Despite calling itself v1.50 it predates the final US v1.40. Contains debug view (L1+R1+L3+R3), early vehicle/character models (e.g. SWAT), different wheels, alternative theme mix, different intro/cutscene cameras & animations, thicker black HUD text outlines. Speculated (not proven) to be a press/preview build for outlets like GameSpot. Context: Redump has a **retail** EU disc that is also v1.50 (SLES-51061, EXE date 2002-10-06, edition "Original"; see 4.2). So "v1.50" was the version number of the first EU retail pressing, and the prototype carries the same number five days before that retail EXE date. | ✅ (build) / ❓ (press-build origin) | [3][4][5][6][38][39][45] |
| Final build ("gold") NA | PS2 | NA | 6 Oct 2002 | — | Final build date given by Hidden Palace. | ⚠️ | [3] |
| Final build ("gold") EU | PS2 | EU | 15 Oct 2002 | — | Final build date given by Hidden Palace; Wikipedia also states development stopped by 15 Oct 2002 when submitted for manufacturing. | ✅ | [1][3] |
| Cut/unused content (retail data) | PS2/PC | — | — | — | Unused weapons in files (Grenade Launcher, Steyr AUG, AK-47 [PC only], Short Python, Tazer, Staplegun, Nailgun, "Silenced Colt"/Wildey, Short MP5, early Uzi/M16, GTA III shotgun); unused "Vice City News" (VCN) radio logo; unused HUD/radio map icons; "MC Tommy" outfit string; tire-slashing stat; cut helicopter-physics cheat; hidden "SA Goon" rating (1000–1005). | ⚠️ | [2] |
| Pre-release media differences | — | — | 2002 (pre-release screenshots/trailers) | — | Grand Theft Wiki (page flagged for cleanup/verification): taser, nailgun, landmines, grenade launcher, Steyr AUG seen in beta; GTA III shotgun/rocket launcher originally present; silenced Ingram on official-site poster; longer-barrel M4; unsilenced PSG-1; early Infernus/Virgo/Blista Compact designs; longer intro; cut Fort Baxter runway, bus shelters, Hyman Memorial Stadium elements; Haitian "I Want YOU" shirt later "RELAX". | ⚠️ | [34] |

Notes:
- No other Vice City prototype (pre-release disc, demo disc or PC beta) is listed on Hidden Palace as of research date; Hidden Palace lists only the Oct 1, 2002 PS2 build plus an EPK, a Take-Two 2003 catalogue and a Double Pack Xbox video (non-prototype material) [3].
- No playable PC/Xbox demo of Vice City was found documented — "not documented".
- TCRF's main Vice City page lists a "Development Info" subpage but no "Proto:" prototype page; Hidden Palace says TCRF has a research article on the Oct 2002 build. The Development Info subpage could not be fetched (site error) — content not verified here.
- The 2025 press coverage disagrees on purpose of the build: Rockstar Intel/GTABoom/Time Extension relay forum speculation that it was a GameSpot/press preview build; jasondeegan.com suggests a developer beta. Treat as unresolved ❓.

Haitian controversy timeline (context for v3.00): Nov 2003 protests by Haitian Centers Council and Haitian Americans for Human Rights in NYC; Mayor Bloomberg threatened legal action; Take-Two apologised and agreed to remove the statements from future copies (GameDeveloper/Gamasutra report dated 10 Dec 2003) [1][20][21].

### 4.3a PC executable builds recognised by modding tools

Community modding toolkits must tell PC executables apart, so their detection code is evidence of which distinct `.exe` builds exist. Plugin-SDK (the main C++ modding SDK for the 3D-era games) recognises exactly three Vice City executables, identified by the address of a known function prologue (`55 89 E5 53`, read as 0x53E58955). This confirms at least these three distinct builds (v1.0, v1.1, Steam); other executables (e.g. Japanese, Rockstar Games Launcher, Mac) exist but are not handled by Plugin-SDK. It gives no file sizes or dates; the gtamods.com exe table (sizes/timestamps) was offline during research.

| Exe build | Plugin-SDK ID | Detection signature (address → value) | Notes | Conf. | Src |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| v1.0 (English) | GAME_10EN (100) | 0x667BF0 → 0x53E58955 | Retail v1.0 | ✅ | 62 |
| v1.1 (English) | GAME_11EN (110) | 0x667C40 → 0x53E58955 | Official patch 1.1 | ✅ | 62 |
| Steam | GAME_STEAM (120) | 0x666BA0 → 0x53E58955 | Steam executable | ✅ | 62 |
| Definitive Edition (Unreal Engine) | GAME_UNREAL | (separate build target GTAVC_UNREAL) | 2021 UE4 version | ✅ | 62 |

### 4.6 Mobile — 10th Anniversary Edition (War Drum Studios port)

Announced 26 Oct 2012, detailed 21 Nov 2012 (GTA Wiki); launch price US$4.99 (UK £2.99 App Store / £3.29 Google Play) [13]. Features: native hi-res graphics, updated character models & lighting, customisable controls [13]. Songs removed vs PS2/PC (licensing): "Billie Jean", "Wanna Be Startin' Somethin'" (Michael Jackson), "Rockit" (Herbie Hancock), "Looking For The Perfect Beat" (Afrika Bambaataa), "The Smurf" (Tyrone Brunson), "Bark at the Moon" (Ozzy Osbourne), "Añunga Ñunga" (Irakere), "Wow" (Kate Bush); "Self-Control" (Laura Branigan) replaces the first-vehicle song [11]. Japanese-version censorship not present on smartphone version (ja.wikipedia) [19].

### 4.8 Other platforms / regional summary

- Dreamcast (unofficial fan port, not a Rockstar release): team led by SKMP (Stefanos Kornilios Mitsis Poiitidis) with Falco Girgis, following their GTA III Dreamcast port; preview reported 24 Feb 2025 (no sound at first), public beta released 2 Apr 2025 (adds 2xFSAA, 24bpp framebuffer mode), runnable on Dreamcast hardware. Underlying codebase (e.g. reVC decompilation) not stated by the article. ⚠️ [40]
- Korean, Russian, Polish and Brazilian editions: only publisher names (KR PC Inplay Interactive; KR mobile Selvas; RU 1C-SoftClub; PL Cenega Classics) are documented; serials/dates not documented [18][22].
- Ratings recorded by Grand Theft Wiki: ESRB M, BBFC 18, ACB MA15+, OFLC (NZ) R18, USK 16+, aDeSe +18, VET/SFB 18, DJCTQ 18 [14]. German digital/10th-Anniversary releases are USK 18 and uncensored (TCRF) [15].
- Compilations containing Vice City (one-line cross-reference only): Grand Theft Auto: Double Pack (PS2/Xbox, 2003), Grand Theft Auto: The Trilogy (2005 Xbox/PS2/PC; 2010 Mac), Rockstar Store "originals" bundle (Dec 2021), and The Definitive Edition (2021) — see compilations and section 10.

### 4.9 Key gaps / unresolved items

1. gtamods.com (exe version table, sizes, entry points for v1.0 / v1.1 / Steam / Japanese / RGL) was still unreachable in the second pass (GTANet "Back soon" maintenance page; Internet Archive "temporarily offline"), so PC exe identification data is still not documented here.
2. PC patch v1.1 date is now 19 May 2003 from one source group (Gamepressure/GRY-Online) ⚠️ and needs independent confirmation.
3. ~~Redump PS2 versions/GH/Platinum~~ resolved in the second pass from Redump disc pages (v1.40 NA, v1.50 EU, v2.01 NA, v2.03 EU, v3.00 NA/EU, v4.00 NA Trilogy, AU v1.00, DE v1.03, JP v1.02, JP Rockstar Classics SLPM-55291). Still open: release dates of GH/Platinum printings, and what changed in v4.00 and in Xbox Rev 1 (TT-115).
4. Steam audio history (Dec 2012 "new audio" depot vs "original audio") contradictory between PCGW, TCRF and user reports.
5. Android: current versions and the German package (com.rockstargames.gtavcger, v1.12) are documented, as is the existence of 1.10. Per-version dates are not documented, and neither is the Fire OS version history.
6. ~~PS4 CUSA / PS3 PSN IDs~~ resolved (CUSA03509/CUSA03546; NPUD20552/NPED00121). EU/AU PS3 release dates are still not documented.
7. Australian-specific content differences (if any) not documented despite separate serial SLES-51316 (Redump: v1.00, EXE 2002-10-22) and the Steam AUS exe depot.
8. NA PS2 release day conflict: 27 Oct 2002 (GTA Wiki, Grand Theft Wiki, Namu, TCRF categories) vs 29 Oct 2002 (Wikipedia, PSXDC, Hidden Palace).
9. No pre-release PC/Xbox build, demo disc or E3 2002 demo build is documented as preserved (Hidden Palace's VC prototype category still holds only the Oct 1, 2002 build).
10. TCRF "Development Info" subpage still unreadable (Cloudflare check). Korean PS2/PC serials and dates are not documented. Mac App Store app IDs and version numbers are not documented.

## 5. Grand Theft Auto: San Andreas (2004)

Developer: Rockstar North (original); TransGaming (Mac OS X port, Cider); War Drum Studios (mobile, 2013–14; TCRF also credits the 2014 UWP version to them) [S3][S4][S2]. The Xbox 360 (2014) and PS3 (2015) versions are built on the mobile port [S2][S5]; their developer credit was not confirmed in fetched sources. Publisher: Rockstar Games (Capcom in Japan, 2007) [S3].
The 2021 *Definitive Edition* is covered in its own section; the PS2/Xbox *Trilogy* compilations are covered in the compilations section (one-line cross-references only here).

Confidence legend: ✅ confirmed by ≥2 independent sources or a primary/database source · ⚠️ single community source · ❓ rumour / unverified.

### 5.2 PlayStation 2 releases and disc revisions

Redump-derived dat (libretro-database `metadat/redump/Sony - PlayStation 2.dat`) lists every verified SA PS2 disc; serial variants sharing one hash are the same disc pressing sold under different catalogue numbers (e.g. budget lines) [S17]. Version strings (v1.00/v1.03/v2.01/v3.00) are the redump-recorded in-disc versions. TCRF calls the post–Hot Coffee build "Version 2 (2.01)" (July 2005) and says it is the build used by the Second Edition, Greatest Hits/Platinum and Trilogy copies [S2] — note redump labels the North American re-release **v3.00**, not 2.01 [S17].

**Note on gtamods.com:** the GTAMods wiki pages (`San_Andreas`, `Gta_sa.exe`) could not be retrieved (first pass: server error / robots block; second pass, 2026-10-24: GTANet "Back soon" maintenance page, no archive.today capture), so exe file sizes and checksums from that wiki are **not reproduced**. Executable identification below comes from the open-source modding SDKs that detect each exe (plugin-sdk, gta-reversed), PCGamingWiki and TCRF.

### 5.10 Known gaps (not documented / could not be verified)

- **gtamods.com exe table** (file sizes/checksums for 1.0 US, 1.0 EU, 1.01, 2.0, 3.0, newsteam r1/r2, RGL) — still not retrieved. On 2026-10-24 gtamods.com showed a GTANet "Back soon" maintenance page; archive.today has no capture of `wiki/Gta_sa.exe`, the Wayback Machine reported no capture for the 2024 URL tried, and further Wayback queries were refused in the browser. Only SDK detection signatures and the gta-reversed "Compact" exe size (5,189,632 B) are recorded. SilentPatch's contributor guide confirms the modding community's working set is "version 1.0 … preferably also the new binaries (newsteam/RGL)" [S24].
- **Exact release days still missing** for: first NA v3.00 pressing (Greatest Hits now dated 20 Feb 2006 by GameFAQs only), German PS2 (v1.00 and v2.01), SLUS-20946P3 Rev 1, Xbox Rev 1 discs (which retail edition each Redump revision belongs to is unknown), the Xbox 360 TT-2232 disc (GameFAQs dates only budget-line SKUs), PS3 TU 1.01. Most newly added reissue dates rest on GameFAQs (user-contributed) alone and are flagged ⚠️; JP Best Price (12 Jul 2007), Rockstar Classics (29 Sep 2011) and PS3 Japan (17 Dec 2015) are confirmed by Kakaku.com.
- **SLPM-55092**: Kakaku.com lists a second "Best Price!" reissue on 23 Jul 2009 without a serial; the link to SLPM-55092 is inferred, not sourced.
- **PS2 Special Edition** date conflict (8 Oct / 17 Oct / 18 Oct / Nov 2005) unresolved.
- **Xbox 360 TU1** rests on one community database (XboxUnity); its Microsoft release date and contents are not documented. **PS3**: only TU 1.01 is documented (no date); whether later PS3 patches exist is unknown (Sony update XML, psdevwiki, serialstation and RPCS3 forum pages were blocked by Cloudflare/TLS). **PS4**: patch 01.01 for CUSA03506 rests on ORBISPatches alone.
- **Windows Phone and Fire OS version histories** — not documented. Rockstar's notes list three 2014 "Android" entries (Fire TV support, controller update, Fire Phone support) whose numbers overlap the Google Play builds; whether these were the Amazon build line is not stated. Android history is otherwise documented from Rockstar's own notes (from v1.03, Dec 2013, to v2.11.311, Jan 2026), with gaps between v1.08 (2015) and v2.0 (2019) and between v2.0 and v2.11.204 (2024).
- **Microsoft Store (Windows 8.1/10) app**: release day differs between sources (14 vs 17 Feb 2014); version history not documented.
- **Korean releases**: no Korean PS2 release found (Redump, PCSX2, GameFAQs); Korean digital releases of the remaster (X360 4 Feb 2015, PS3 2 Dec 2015) rest on GameFAQs only.
- **Prototypes**: Hidden Palace lists only the Xbox 8 Mar 2005 debug build (dumped, not released) and the Aug/Sep 2014 Steam beta-branch builds; no PS2 or PC prototype disc. The Feb 2025 partial-code leak remains unverified. TCRF could not be re-checked (Cloudflare challenge).

## 6. Handheld & Spin-off Era

> Scope: Grand Theft Auto Advance (GBA), Liberty City Stories, Vice City Stories, Chinatown Wars, cancelled handheld projects, and the PSN and mobile ports of the handheld games. Compilations are cross-referenced only (see Section 12). Earlier handheld ports (GTA 1 and GTA 2 on Game Boy Color) belong in Sections 1–2.
>
> **How the disc and cartridge data was gathered:** serials and version strings marked "dat" come from the No-Intro (cartridge, PSN) and Redump (UMD, DVD) DAT files as mirrored in the libretro-database GitHub repository (DAT versions dated 2026-10). The redump.org and datomatic.no-intro.org websites refused automated access during this research, so the individual disc pages could not be opened. Each DAT entry is one verified dump of a physical or digital copy. Where Redump lists the same serial more than once, the entries are different pressings (different hashes); the table shows the suffix printed on the disc (e.g. `/P` = Platinum, `GH` = Greatest Hits).

### 6.1 Grand Theft Auto Advance (Game Boy Advance, 2004)

The box and title screen say only **"Grand Theft Auto"**. Developed by **Digital Eclipse** and published by **Rockstar Games**. The story is a prequel to GTA III, set in Liberty City and played from a top-down view [1][3].

#### 6.1.1 Development history and prototypes

| Version/Build | Platform | Region | Release date | Serial / ID / Version | Notes | Conf. | Src |

| Pre-Crawfish GBA "GTA 3" attempt | GBA | – | not documented (before 2002) | none | A former Crawfish developer said "There was one before ours that also got canned". No developer, footage or details are known. | ❓ | [2] |
| *Grand Theft Auto III* for GBA, announced | GBA | – | 2001 (announced) | none | Announced by **Destination Software** in 2001 after it licensed the rights from Take-Two/Rockstar. Lost Media Archive names Digital Worldwide as the intended publisher; Unseen64 names Destination Software. | ⚠️ | [2][4] |
| Crawfish Interactive *GTA 3* GBA (cancelled) | GBA | – | Planned for late 2002, then 2003 | not documented | Developed by **Crawfish Interactive**. Cancellation rumours appeared in Nov 2002; Destination denied them. About a week later Crawfish closed and laid off its staff (GTA Wiki: Crawfish "went defunct in November 2002"). Story per ex-developer Dave Murphy: set a few months before GTA III, with a different protagonist (Lost Media Archive says Claude was planned) who works for the Mafia. Four planned multiplayer modes: Liberty City Survivor, City Circuit, Car Jack Crazy and Special Delivery. | ✅ | [2][3][4] |
| Crawfish early tech-demo/prototype footage | GBA | – | Footage published 2016-12 | not documented | **PtoPOnline** published video of an early Crawfish prototype or tech demo, with few features. Lost Media Archive: a copy exists but has **not been released** publicly. Hidden Palace identifies the build in an unlisted PtoPOnline video as the **Apr 12, 2002** prototype (above); whether this is the same 2016 video is not stated. | ⚠️ | [2][4][52] |
| Transfer to Digital Eclipse | GBA | – | after Nov 2002 | – | Rockstar moved the project to Digital Eclipse. Rockstar's Susan Cummings arranged it through earlier contacts, and the game was made "in collaboration with Rockstar North". The GTA III port concept was dropped in favour of an original prequel story. Ex-Crawfish staff say some Crawfish elements survived, but not the controls or multiplayer. Destination Software kept claiming to be publisher for about a year but did not publish the final game. | ✅ | [2][3] |
| Final-game debug content | GBA | – | – | – | TCRF documents a debug cheat mode (A+B+Start shows "CHEAT MODE ON" and the map coordinates), a hidden chapter and stage select (main menu: ←→↑↓ L R, hold Start + A), and an unused barrel HUD icon. | ⚠️ | [5] |

> **Note on the "Rockstar Leeds" premise:** no source found credits Rockstar Leeds with any GBA GTA work. Every source checked names Crawfish Interactive (cancelled GTA III port) and then Digital Eclipse (final game).
>
> **Disambiguation:** *Advance GTA* (AGB-AG7J-JPN, MTO, Japan, 2001-03-21) is an **unrelated** racing game [6].
>
> **Prototypes:** two Crawfish builds are documented on Hidden Palace: 2002-04-12 (undumped) and 2002-04-16 (released; also on TCRF). No prototype of the final Digital Eclipse game is documented in No-Intro, on TCRF or on Hidden Palace (a full-text HP search for "Grand Theft Auto Advance" returned no pages, checked 2026-10-24).

### 6.2 Grand Theft Auto: Liberty City Stories (PSP 2005 · PS2 2006 · PS3 PSN 2013 · iOS 2015 · Android/Fire OS 2016)

Developed by **Rockstar Leeds** with **Rockstar North**. Published by Rockstar Games, and by **Capcom** in Japan. It was the first game on Rockstar's new in-house engine, replacing RenderWare (IGN preview of 2005-09-09, cited by Wikipedia) [13][14][15].

### 6.3 Grand Theft Auto: Vice City Stories (PSP 2006 · PS2 2007 · PS3 PSN 2013)

Developed by **Rockstar Leeds** with **Rockstar North**. Published by Rockstar Games, and by **Capcom** in Japan [34][35][36][37].

#### 6.3.1 Development and pre-release

| Version/Build | Platform | Region | Release date | Serial / ID / Version | Notes | Conf. | Src |

### 6.4 Grand Theft Auto: Chinatown Wars (DS 2009 · PSP 2009 · iOS 2010 · iPad HD 2010 · Android/Fire OS 2014)

Developed by **Rockstar Leeds** with **Rockstar North**. Published by Rockstar Games, and in Japan by **Cyberfront** (DS). The Android port, and per Wikipedia's footnotes the 2014 iOS re-port, are credited to **War Drum Studios** [40][41][42].

| Fire OS | Fire OS | WW | **2014-12-18** (Wikipedia groups it with Android) | Amazon ASIN not documented | Rockstar lists one Amazon update: **Amazon v1.01** (2015-02-24): sharper UI, art and fonts for high-resolution screens; tutorial prompts updated for TV devices such as Fire TV; bug fixes. | ⚠️ (launch date) / ✅ (v1.01) | [40][57] |

> **Cross-reference:** compilations and bundles that include the handheld games, if any, are covered in Section 12. No Redump or No-Intro entry found here is a multi-game compilation. GameFAQs lists a PS2 product "Grand Theft Auto: Liberty City Stories / Vice City Stories" dated 2009-06-23 [60] — for Section 12 (not verified further here).

### 6.6 Gaps and open items (Section 6)

- **Redump and No-Intro web pages** (disc pages with EXE dates, barcodes, EDC, ring codes) could not be opened; serials, version strings, sizes and CRCs come from the DAT files mirrored on GitHub. On-sale dates per LCS pressing (v2.00, v3.00) remain **not documented**; only their build dates (Hidden Palace) are known. Most reissue dates added in the second pass (Platinum, Greatest Hits, Best Price, Rockstar Classics, PSN Store dates) come from GameFAQs (user-contributed) alone and are flagged ⚠️; only the LCS PSP Best Price! date (2008-07-24) is confirmed by a second source (Kakaku.com).
- **LCS launch dates conflict:** Wikipedia and others give 2005-10-25 (NA) / 2005-11-04 (EU); Hidden Palace and GameFAQs give 2005-10-24 / 2005-10-28. Unresolved.
- **Japanese serial roles** are now attributed via GameFAQs (original vs Best Price / Best Hits for LCS PSP & PS2, VCS PSP & PS2, CTW PSP); apart from the LCS PSP Best Price date, these are single-source.
- **Hidden Palace** pages give build dates, UMD labels, dumpers/leakers and original filenames but **no hashes**. Several LCS, VCS and CTW prototypes are listed as "Dumped, not released". The VCS Sep 24, 2006 prototype's releaser is unrecorded on HP.
- **TCRF** (tcrf.net and new.tcrf.net) could not be opened (Cloudflare challenge / navigation refused), so TCRF's VCS `Proto:` page and the CTW pages remain unchecked.
- **Chinatown Wars:** the iPad HD app ID (id386183589) is found, but its version history is not (the App Store page returns an error). The Fire OS ASIN is still not documented. The HD app's retirement date conflicts (Dec 2014 vs the store text's 3 Apr 2015). The Korean PSP release (ULKS-46223, 2009-11-02) and the Japanese publisher (CyberFront) rest on GameFAQs only.
- **LCS/VCS on PS4/PS5:** no release found (US PlayStation Store search, web search); GameFAQs marks the VCS PS4 Store entry "Canceled". This shows absence only, not proof.
- **Korean releases:** LCS Korea (ULKS-46157) still has no documented date. VCS Korea (2007-02-28) has no known serial.
- **Android versions:** now documented from Rockstar's own update notes (LCS from v1.9, 2016; CTW from v1.01, 2015). Launch-day version numbers and the gaps between the 2016–2019 and 2023+ builds are not documented.

### 7.5 PC patch history — Grand Theft Auto IV (GFWL era, 2008–2016)

Rockstar shipped region-specific numbering: EFIGS (English/French/Italian/German/Spanish), Russian and Japanese builds carried different version numbers for the same "Patch N" Patch 5 (1.0.5.0) merged the region SKUs into a single all-language executable, yet Rockstar continued to publish separate Russian and Japanese version numbers through Patch 8. The Rockstar Support page dates shown today (e.g. 2024/2026) are page-update dates, not release dates.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

GTA Wiki's version table calls the PC release "Version 1.00". **"1.0.0.4" is not the launch build**: it is the label Rockstar used in its 2009-11-10 announcement of Patch 5 ("Today we released patched 1.0.0.4…"), i.e. a mislabel of 1.0.5.0 (GTA Wiki; Cheat Code Central also reports the Nov 2009 patch as 1.0.0.4) | Original 2-DVD release (GFWL + SecuROM) | ⚠️ (1.0.0.0: single wiki source) / ✅ (1.0.0.4 = Patch 5 label) | 11, 67, 76, 77 |

### 7.10 Compilations (cross-reference only)

GTA IV's only first-party bundle in scope is *The Complete Edition* (7.7/7.9). Any other multi-game compilations containing GTA IV are covered in the compilations section of the master document.

### 7.11 Known gaps (searched, not documented in this pass)

- PC retail launch exe 1.0.0.0 rests on a single wiki statement (GTA Wiki); no primary source (e.g. exe properties from a retail disc dump) retrieved. EFLC PC launch version "1.1.0.0" still unverified ❓.
- Complete Edition builds: release dates of 1.2.0.43 and 1.2.0.59 (1.2.0.59 only attested by a wiki editor note), the build number behind the "1.20" hotfixes (2020-03-24/31), and what "1.2.0.30" was — Rockstar Support (old articles gone, excluded from Wayback), SteamDB (bot check) and GTAForums/gtamods (bot check / offline) not retrievable.
- Japanese and Russian PC version numbers for Patches 1–5 (JP fan wiki's front-page strings contradict Rockstar numbering and were not used).
- Xbox 360 title updates: release dates and contents of individual TUs v1–v8 (only the list, sizes and base versions are known); mapping between TU numbers and GTA Wiki's "1.01–1.06". PS3: individual dates of 1.01–1.06 and a reliable date for 1.07 (GTA Wiki's 2011-03-13 conflicts with its GameSpy-removal content); Sony update XML (BLUS30127-ver.xml etc.) and serialstation not reachable.
- Korean Xbox 360 release date; meaning of Hidden Palace's "KO 2012-02-15"; PS3 USA Special Edition BLUS-30149 and Korean EFLC BLKS-20198 only in the libretro DB (not in Redump); which Japanese Rockstar Classics CE serial (BLJM-60525 vs BLJM-61180) matches the 2014-04-24 release; release dates of the other budget reprints (Platinum, Greatest Hits, Classics, Essentials).
- Exact Internet Archive upload/takedown dates of the 2007 prototype; TCRF prerelease pages (Cloudflare bot check).
- *Audit integration:* the Xbox 360 dev/test-kit leftovers (Sep 10, 2007 and Feb 29, 2008 data, Last Area Assets) and the German EPK were added (7.2 / 7.1). Still missing: a file release date for "Last Area Assets", and which console each leftover came from (dev kit or test kit, as Hidden Palace labels them).
- Xbox Series X|S compatibility rests on GTA Wiki only; German USK/other regional rating edits beyond Australia.

## 8. Grand Theft Auto V — Platforms, Editions, Title Updates & PC Builds

> Scope: GTA V as a product (all platforms), its retail/digital editions, the console title-update (TU) numbering and the PC executable build numbers. GTA Online content-update *descriptions* live in Section 9; here each TU/build is only mapped to the name of the update it shipped with. Compilations are cross-referenced in Section 12.

### 8.1 Development history & pre-release milestones

| Date | Event | Conf. | Src |
|---|---|---|---|
| c. 2008-04 | Preliminary work began around the release of *GTA IV*; full development lasted about three years. | ✅ | [1] |
| — | Engine: proprietary RAGE (Rockstar Advanced Game Engine), overhauled for draw distance; Euphoria (animation) and Bullet (physics) middleware. | ✅ | [1] |
| — | Team: Rockstar North core team ≈360; >1,000 people across Rockstar Leeds, Lincoln, London, New England, San Diego, Toronto. | ✅ | [1] |
| — | Budget estimates: >US$137 m development (analyst Arvind Bhatia); >£170 m (US$265 m) dev + marketing (The Scotsman, Marty McLaughlin). Estimates, not official. | ⚠️ | [1] |
| — | Cut concepts: an early three-city San Andreas setting was dropped for Los Santos alone; three separate stories were merged into interconnected narratives. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2011-10-25 | Rockstar confirms the game. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2011-11-02 | Debut trailer. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2012-11-14 | Second trailer (planned for 2 Nov, delayed by Hurricane Sandy). | ✅ | [1] |
| 2013-01-31 | Release moved from the projected spring (March–May) 2013 window to 2013-09-17. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2013-03-31 / 2013-04-02 | Cover-art mural in Manhattan; digital artwork released 2 Apr. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2013-04-26 | Three protagonist trailers. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2013-07-09 | First gameplay trailer. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2013-07-15 | Japanese release date (2013-10-10) announced. | ✅ | [16] |
| 2013-08-15 | *Grand Theft Auto Online* gameplay trailer. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2013-08 | Some European PS3 pre-order users could download parts of the game (soundtrack, dialogue) from a pre-load file; Sony removed it and apologised. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2013-08-25 | Game submitted for manufacturing (development ceased / went gold). | ✅ | [1] |
| 2013-08-29 | Final pre-launch trailer. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2014-06-09 (E3 2014) | PS4 / Xbox One / PC re-release announced; pre-orders from 2014-06-10. | ✅ | [1][16] |
| 2014–2015 | PC version delayed from launching with the 2014 console re-release → 2015-01-27 → 2015-03-24 → 2015-04-14. | ✅ | [1] |
| 2020-06-11 | PS5 / Xbox Series X\|S version announced for "late 2021"; later dated 2021-11-11, then delayed to March 2022, with 2022-03-15 fixed in Feb 2022 (Wikipedia). GTA Wiki instead says the firm date was announced 2021-05-19. | ✅ / ⚠️ (date-setting conflict) | [1][16] |
| 2023-12-24 | GTA V source code reportedly leaked online (alleged link to Lapsus$ members). Dataminers subsequently found at least eight cancelled single-player DLC sets incl. a Liberty City expansion. | ✅ | [2] |

**Leaked / preserved pre-release builds.** *(Corrected in the audit-integration pass: an earlier draft said no pre-release GTA V build with a documented build number had leaked. That is out of date.)* Hidden Palace documents the builds, DLC prototypes and dev-kit leftovers below. All rows rest on that one community archive, so they are ⚠️. Hidden Palace's own "build dates" are not compile dates for the DLC items (they come from the Xbox CDN package modification dates) or for the 388.5 patch (latest file modification). Full cross-game index: §11.7.

| Build date (per Hidden Palace) | Item | Platform | Build name / version | Origin & release | Notes | Conf. | Src |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2013-05-07 | **DLC Weapon 5** prototype | Xbox 360 | "DLC Weapon 5" | PartnerNet package on the Xbox CDN; dated from the package modification date | Contains an early Assault SMG model ("Assault Sub Machinegun"). Works only on the unpatched retail game. Hidden Palace's download file is labelled "Jul 13, 2013". | ⚠️ | [66] |
| 2013-05-07 | **DLC Weapon 7** prototype | Xbox 360 | — | PartnerNet / Xbox CDN | Early Bullpup Shotgun model. That weapon later shipped with the Collector's Edition content. | ⚠️ | [66] |
| 2013-05-20 | **DLC Weapon 1** prototype | Xbox 360 | — | PartnerNet / Xbox CDN | Heavy Rifle, which was cut and later added to current-gen GTA Online in *The Contract*. | ⚠️ | [66] |
| 2013-05-20 | **DLC Weapon 2** prototype | Xbox 360 | "DLC Weapon 2" | PartnerNet / Xbox CDN | "Programmable Assault Rifle" (a cut weapon; the page's quoted modder calls it the Programmable Airburst Rifle). | ⚠️ | [66] |
| 2013-05-20 | **DLC Weapon 3** prototype | Xbox 360 | "DLC Weapon 3" | PartnerNet / Xbox CDN | Assault MG (cut weapon). | ⚠️ | [66] |
| 2013-07-04 | **DLC Weapon 6** prototype | Xbox 360 | — | PartnerNet / Xbox CDN | Rubber Gun (cut weapon). | ⚠️ | [66] |
| 2013-07-04 | **Assault Sniper DLC** prototype | Xbox 360 | — | PartnerNet / Xbox CDN | Assault Sniper (cut weapon). | ⚠️ | [66] |
| 2013-07-04 | **Atomic Blimp DLC** prototype | Xbox 360 | — | PartnerNet / Xbox CDN | Atomic Blimp vehicle. Hidden Palace says the *retail* package was modified on 2013-07-27. | ⚠️ | [66] |
| 2013-09-13 | **Patch Data prototype (TU 1.01)** | PS3 (BLES01807) | **377** | Recovered from PS3 dev-kit HDD `TB00022`; dumped and released by MrPinball64 in 2023; extracted by funtimeandrefoxy on 2025-02-25 | Hidden Palace places build 377 between the base retail build **369** and the retail 1.01 build **388.2**. See §8.5. | ⚠️ | [67] |
| 2013-10-03 | **Patch Data prototype (TU 1.02)** | PS3 (BLES01807) | **388.5** | Recovered from a PS3 dev-kit HDD; dumped and released by "the cnh" on 2024-04-14; extracted 2025-02-12 | Corresponds to TU 1.02, whose retail version Hidden Palace says is 388.6. The retail 1.02 package itself is **lost**. The EBOOT.BIN is debug-signed. Hidden Palace notes that the build date comes from the latest file modification date and is "inaccurate". | ⚠️ | [67] |
| 2014-10-13 | **"Oct 13, 2014" development build**: GTA5_NG QA Version 108 | "Other" on Hidden Palace (binaries and baked assets for all platforms) | **0.0.0.77**; QA version **108** | Came from the "Build" directory taken in the September 2022 Rockstar intrusion (§11.3). It was sold privately and then leaked by an unknown buyer. File release date **2026-09-18** ("four year anniversary of the 2022 leaks"). | Predates the PC launch by about 6 months. Has debug keyboards. Contains a very early rendition of the GTA VI map (`-level env_test`), which Hidden Palace says shows Cayo Perico was partly cut GTA VI content. Also has assets for a cut Liberty City expansion and the cut "Agent Trevor" DLC. The newest map files were exported around 2014-12-19, later than the binary. | ⚠️ (single community source; recent) | [64] |
| 2020-09-30 | **"GTA5 Debug.exe" + "GTA5 Debug.pdb"** ("Leftover Sep 30, 2020 Data") | PC | — | Hidden Palace; origin not stated | Only those two files. The date comes from the executable. | ⚠️ | [68] |
| c. 2022-09-16 | **Source-code build 1.0.2699.0** ("Sep 16, 2022 build") | PC | **1.0.2699.0** | Released by "Lapsus$" according to Hidden Palace; this is the December 2023 source leak (§11.6) | Code only; assets have to be copied from a retail copy. Rockstar's build tools were included. Only the "Debug" configuration is known to work (Hidden Palace also mentions a "Bank Release" compile). Hidden Palace treats it as separate from the Oct 13, 2014 build. The build name is the same as the retail PC executable 1.0.2699.0 (*The Criminal Enterprises*, 2022-07-26; §8.6). | ⚠️ | [65] |

**PS3 dev-kit leftovers (assets, not runnable builds)** ⚠️ [68]:
- *cnh devkit leftovers assets.* A Rockstar / Team Bondi PS3 dev kit (model 1400a) was put on the Internet Archive on 2024-04-14. It held wiped remnants of builds from about April 2010 to May 2012, July 2013 "Install" builds, and two patches dated around 2013-10-03.
- *Cloud Cache Files.* Extracted on 2025-07-14 from dev kit `1400a 2454`: 19 cloud-cache .dat files retrieved in November 2014 and related to Heists. More than 200 further files, dated around September 2013, came from dev kit `TB00022`.
- *CarsonGT Extracted Assets.* PS3 audio, video and RPF fragments, including a June 2013 `scaleform_generic.rpf` with a different loading screen.

| PC "Enhanced" / PC "Legacy" split | Windows | WW | 2025-03-04 | Free upgrade bringing PS5/XSX content and features (ray tracing etc.) to PC as a separate "Enhanced" product; original PC version continues as "Legacy". GTA+ came to PC with Enhanced. | ✅ | [2][5][7] |

### 8.5 Console title updates (game & GTA Online version numbers)

**How the numbering works.** Rockstar labels each patch "Title Update 1.xx". From launch until the PS4/XB1 release, the story-mode and Online versions were the same (1.01–1.17). The PS4/XB1 re-release started its own story-mode count at 1.01 (paired with Online 1.17); the PC version also started at 1.01 but is usually identified by its executable build (§8.6). Each platform kept its own numbers until **1.29 (2015-09-15)**. From then on one TU number has covered PS4, XB1 and PC, and later PS5/XSX and PC Enhanced too. Rockstar Support's "Title Update 1.27/1.28" note pages list PS3/X360/PS4/XB1/PC together under the Online number. The GTA Wiki gives separate per-platform game versions for those two patches (e.g. 1.24 for PS3/X360, 1.11 for PS4/XB1, PC 350.3 for 1.27). [3][20][21][22][40]

### 8.5b PlayStation package (patch) versions — PS4 & PS5

Sony's package version numbers (`01.xx` on PS4, `01.0xx.000` on PS5) are **not** the same as Rockstar's TU numbers.
- **PS4 dates** are ORBISPatches' package **creation dates**. Packages are built a few days before release, so these are not release dates.
- **PS5 dates** are ProsperoPatches' **"Date Added"**, the time the tracker first saw the package. The same package version is often re-issued with a new date for later hotfixes.
- **Associated update** is ORBISPatches' own description text for each package; no mapping was inferred.

ORBISPatches lists the same versions and creation dates for the US (CUSA00419) and EU (CUSA00411) PS4 SKUs; the EU list lacks some pre-2015-10 entries. [12][14][48][49] ✅

Its list runs from 01.19 (2017-04-03) to **01.54** (2026-07-09, Kortz Center Heist), and mostly the same creation dates as the matching US packages. The exceptions are a JP-only extra package on 2019-07-26 and dates one day off in 2022 (2022-08-31 and 2022-12-09). [48] ✅

The disc-content-ID SKUs **PPSA04264** (US) and **PPSA04263** (EU) carry the same packages, but ProsperoPatches' history for them starts later: PPSA04264 from 01.005.000 (first seen 2023-10-07), PPSA04263 from 01.006.000 (2024-01-11). ProsperoPatches has no page for PPSA04265 (JP); requesting it returns the site home page. The "dates first seen" match the dates Rockstar/GTA Wiki give for the corresponding PS5 TU drops in §8.5. [14][49] ✅

### 8.6 PC (Legacy) executable builds — `GTA5.exe` 1.0.xxx.x

**Non-retail PC builds:** a leftover "GTA5 Debug.exe" + .pdb dated 2020-09-30 (not in the list below), and the leaked source code, which Hidden Palace catalogues as build **1.0.2699.0** (code c. 2022-09-16). That number is the same as the retail *Criminal Enterprises* executable 1.0.2699.0 (2022-07-26) in the list below. Hidden Palace's code date is about seven weeks after that release; the reason is not documented. Both items rest on Hidden Palace only; see §8.1. ⚠️ [65][68]

**Sources and method.** The build list below is the complete set of `GTA5.exe` versions enumerated by the Script Hook V .NET `GameVersion` enum [19]. That enum mirrors the values Alexander Blade's Script Hook V returns from `getGameVersion()`, so it is effectively the modding community's canonical list of retail PC executables. It agrees with the Script Hook V readme's supported-versions list [33], and the Script Hook V download page gives the latest as 1.0.3889.0 [34].
- **Associated update:** taken from FiveM's official `sv_enforceGameBuild` table, which maps build numbers to DLC/"marketing names" [35], or from the GTA Wiki TU pages for 2015 builds [3][20]–[25].
- **Dates:** a build tied to a named update takes that update's release date from §8.5. "SHVDN support" is the date the build was added to the SHVDN enum (git history [19]). It is an *upper bound* on the release date, not the release date itself.
- **Inferred mappings:** where no source ties a build to a TU, the row says "Inferred from ordering — not sourced ❓". Treat these as unverified. A second pass (2026-10) found no PC build numbers on any GTA Wiki per-update page after 2015-07-21 (all 57 post-2015 pages were checked), nor on Rockstar Support notes or news coverage. Script Hook V's readme changelog lists versions without dates.
- **PC-only patch dates that no build is tied to.** GTA Wiki lists these PC Legacy-only patches [3]: 2015-04-14 (1.01), 2018-10-16 (1.45), 2020-09-09 (1.52), 2022-10-25 (1.63), 2023-02-01 (1.66), 2023-10-03 (1.67), 2024-04-16 (1.68), 2024-09-17, 2024-09-25, 2024-10-15, 2024-10-30 (1.69), 2024-12-13, 2025-02-19 (1.70), 2025-07-08 (1.71), 2026-03-17 (1.72), 2026-07-30 (1.73). Each hotfix build without a date below presumably matches one of these, but which one is not sourced.
- **Steam/NoSteam:** up to 1.0.2699.0 Rockstar shipped separate Steam and non-Steam executables, and Epic had one variant (1.0.1868.4 EGS). From 1.0.2699.16 onward SHVDN lists a single executable per build [19]. ⚠️

**FiveM-packaged builds (`GTA5.exe` sizes from FiveM's GameCache).** FiveM can switch players to these builds, and its launcher stores the matching `GTA5.exe` and `update.rpf` for each [36]. Each entry gives build, exe size in bytes, exe SHA-1 (first 8 chars):
1604 — 72,484,280 (8939c8c7) · 2060 — 60,589,184 (741c8b91) · 2189 — 63,124,096 (fcd5fd8a) · 2372 — 59,716,912 (470235e0) · 2545 — 59,988,376 (517556bb) · 2612 — 60,351,952 (d423086f) · 2699 — 61,111,680 (b9f3960c) · 2802 — 46,709,592 (ebb6c144) · 2944 — 49,828,848 (4d968a07) · 3095 — 49,634,800 (17a074bb) · 3179 — 55,367,152 (cf2b853a) · 3258 — 56,066,032 (17183412) · 3323 — 57,496,560 (059bcf06) · 3407 — 54,341,608 (36019819) · 3570 — 49,242,152 (17d59395) · 3751 — 49,183,352 (e5fe7bd7) · 3788 — 49,221,752 (82b4575d) · 3889 — 47,467,128 (a9bff696). FiveM also supports "build 1" (base game without DLC) and RedM build 1491 (for RDR2, outside scope). ✅ [35][36]

### 8.7 PC Enhanced executable builds (2025–present)

The Enhanced edition (2025-03-04) has its own build numbering, starting at 811/812. Script Hook V describes these as **1.0.xxxx.yy** (e.g. "1.0.1158.13"). SHVDN-Enhanced's `ExeVersionConsts` reads the executable file version as **2.0.xxxx.yy** (e.g. `new(2, 0, 811, 8)`). The 811–1013 builds are listed in SHVDN-Enhanced [38]; 1013.34 is named in a commit message and 1158.13 comes from Script Hook V [34].
