| GTA VI — **Ultimate Edition Upgrade** (separate digital add-on), the **one month of GTA+** bundled with digital pre-orders, and **no physical Ultimate Edition** | §11.2 | Rockstar Support "Grand Theft Auto VI Platforms, Editions, and Versions" (last updated 2026-08-18). Ultimate Edition = game + "GTAVI: Ultimate Edition Upgrade" ('95 Grotti Cheetah, Hawk & Little Morgan revolvers, Rideout Customs, Classic Car Collection and more). Standard owners, including physical-code owners, can buy the Upgrade on PS/Microsoft stores after redeeming. Digital pre-orders before 2026-11-20 get the Vintage Vice City Pack **plus one month of GTA+**; the physical code includes the Vintage Vice City Pack only. PlayStation codes are region-locked (Brazil / LATAM / NA / Korea / Japan / UK / Saudi Arabia & UAE / Rest of Asia / Rest of EMEA & Asia); Xbox codes are not region-locked. A note that these unofficial jars exist would stop readers mistaking them for missing official releases. | ⚠️ | [13] |

### 14.3 Errors found

No wrong release date, serial or version number was found in the ~150 values spot-checked (see 14.4). The problems below are out-of-date statements, internal inconsistencies between sections, and one conflicting data point.

| Section | Existing value | Correct value | Src |
|---|---|---|---|
| §8.1 ("Leaked / preserved pre-release builds"), §8.10 gaps ("Still not documented: a playable leaked GTA V pre-release dev build") and §11.6 ("Pre-release GTA V builds: None with documented build numbers leaked") | No pre-release GTA V build with a documented build number has leaked | Out of date. Hidden Palace documents a GTA V development build dated 2014-10-13 (GTA5_NG QA Version 108 / 0.0.0.77), released 2026-09-18; PS3 patch prototypes, builds 377 and 388.5; and the leaked source as build 1.0.2699.0 (code c. 2022-09-16). | [3][4][6] |
| §11.7 index, row "Race'n'Chase / GTA (1997) — two very early prototypes" | Build date "not documented" | §1.1 already gives the dates **11 Nov 1994** (GTA ProtoType 1) and **2 Feb 1995** (ProtoType 2, `DINO.EXE`), both on Hidden Palace. | [2] (and §1 sources) |
| §11.7 index, row "GTA (1997) PC demos" | Build date "not documented" | §1.2 gives Hidden Palace build dates of **1 Sep 1997** (ECTS/magazine demos), **11 Nov 1997** (8-bit / 24-bit / 3dfx "Hot Bitch Teaser") and **4 Mar 1998** (DOS demo). | [2] |
| §11.7 index, row "GTA: London 1969 — TCRF 'Prototype' page (Windows), contents not retrievable" | Platform Windows; date not documented | The only preserved London 1969 pre-release build Hidden Palace knows of is a **PlayStation** PAL CD-R, "PAL PSX BETA 37", built **11 Apr 1999** and undumped (already in §1.7). | [2] |
| §11.7 index (whole table) and §11.8 row "*GTA: Liberty City Stories* early concepts: only the three 2005 prototypes (11.7) are documented" | The index lists 3 LCS prototypes and no VCS, CW, GTA 2 PS1/Palm, GTA PS1, SA, GTA IV DLC/patch or GTA V builds | Sections 1–7 now document many more Hidden Palace builds: LCS 2005-05-28/29, 07-26, 09-01, 09-11, 09-23, 10-07, 10-22 and 2006-03-22; VCS 2006-08-08, 09-01, 09-24, 10-04; CW PSP 2009-07-28, 08-26, 08-28, 08-31, 09-15; GTA 2 PS1 1999-09-10 and Palm OS demos; GTA PS1 1998-05-23; SA Xbox 2005-03-08 and Steam 2014-08-28/09-02; GTA IV 2008-05-26, 2008-08-19, 2009-01-30 (×2), 2009-09-24; EFLC 2009-09-29. Add the GTA V builds from 14.2 as well. The index and the 11.8 wording are stale. | [2] |
| §11.8 row "GTA 2 — Miami expansion" | Described as a Miami-set GTA 2 *expansion* | GTA Wiki, citing Obbe Vermeij's blog (2023-11-11), describes the Dundee project as ***Grand Theft Auto 2.5*** ("GTA Miami"): a separate game with an isometric "2.5-dimensional" top-down 3D view, cancelled when DMA's Dundee office closed. Show both descriptions and flag the conflict. | [10] |
| §7.7 "Complete Edition conversion" dated **2020-03-19** | — (not an error; extra data point) | Steam's own store API gives the **release_date** of app 12210 ("Grand Theft Auto IV: The Complete Edition") as **Mar 24, 2020**. That is the date of the first "1.20" hotfix §7.7 records; the API date is probably store metadata rather than the conversion day. Add it as a conflicting value. | [21] |
### 14.4 Checked and confirmed present

**Completeness, per master list**

- **Wikipedia series tables** [1]: all 8 main-series games, 4 expansions, 4 handhelds and 8 compilations/remasters (24 titles), and every platform named in those tables, are covered.
- **GTA Wiki** [11]: the series article's game list (17 titles incl. London 1969/1961 and EFLC), the GTA V / GTA Online / SA "Editions" pages, and the cancelled-project pages (Sega Saturn GTA, Grand Theft Auto 64, Online Crime World, GTA III GBA) are present. Missing: the Barking Dog *GTA: Online* proposal and the "GTA 2.5" description (14.2 / 14.3), plus the iFruit and Manual apps (14.2).
- **GTA Online updates (GTA Wiki "Updates in GTA Online")** [11]: all **46** titled updates from Beach Bum (2013-11-19) to The Kortz Center Heist (2026-07-14) are in §9, and their dates agree. The one difference is Capture: §9 has 17 Dec 2013 from Rockstar Newswire, GTA Wiki has 18 Dec. §9 also has entries GTA Wiki's list lacks (Festive Surprise 2016, Deadline, GTA+, shutdown).
- **MobyGames group** [20]: 50 entries. All are present except the 6 compilation/SKU listings and the iFruit (PS Vita) entry given in 14.2.
- **Hidden Palace** [2]: 56 canonical build pages. **46 present** in sections 1–7: all GTA 1, London 1969, GTA 2, GTA III (GBA and PAL v1.40), VC, SA, LCS, VCS, CW and GTA IV/EFLC builds. The VC Oct 2, 2002 and CW Sep 16, 2009 pages are redirects to the Oct 1 / Sep 15 pages, which are present. Missing: every GTA V page and the GTA IV leftover/EPK asset pages (14.2).
- **TCRF** category could not be read (Cloudflare bot check in the browser; WebFetch returned only the heading). Sections 1–7 already cite many TCRF pages directly.
- **DAT serials** (`gta_dat_entries.md`) [12][13][14][15]: **214** unique IDs. 160 matched directly on the first pass. Another 44 are present in a different form: box-code suffixes, PSN content IDs of serials already listed, or `TT-` serials the pattern missed. That left 10 absent from the per-game sections. During this audit the section editors added 8 of them: NPUD20062, NPED00063, NPUD20552, SLPM-55291, TT-014, TT-015, TT-115 (incl. VC Xbox Rev 1) and BLUS-41019 (now in §3, §4, §12, §13). **2 remain missing**: BLKS-20442 and NPJB00516 (14.2). The J2ME jars have no serials (see 14.2).
- **Mobile platforms per game** (Wikipedia infobox platforms [1][22]): GTA III (iOS, Android, Fire OS), VC (iOS, Android, Fire OS), SA (iOS, Android, Windows Phone, Fire OS), LCS (iOS, Android, Fire OS), CW (iOS, iPad HD, Android, Fire OS) and Trilogy DE (iOS, Android via Netflix and standalone) are all covered, with release dates matching Wikipedia. No official J2ME/Java GTA exists (see the J2ME row in 14.2).
- **Backward compatibility / subscriptions:** GTA IV + EFLC on Xbox One BC (2017-02-09) and SA X360 on Xbox One BC (2018-06-07) are both in their sections and match the Wikipedia BC list [23]. GTA V on PS Now, Game Pass and PS Plus is in §8.4.

**Error spot-check (Task B), all consistent with the cited source**

- **Release dates vs Wikipedia infoboxes** [22]: 60 values (GTA III 4, VC 7, SA 11, GTA IV/TLAD/TBoGT/EFLC/CE 10, CW 9, LCS 8, VCS 6, GTA Advance 2, GTA 2 3). All 60 present and matching. Where sections give two dates, Wikipedia's value is one of them (e.g. LCS PSP NA 24/25 Oct 2005; VCS PS2 NA 5/6 Mar 2007).
- **GTA Online update dates vs GTA Wiki**: 46 checked, as above.
- **GTA V PC builds vs FiveM `sv_enforceGameBuild` table** [24]: 17 build→update mappings (1604 Arena War … 3889 Kortz Center Heist, plus 2612 `mpg9ec` and the Casino/Casino Heist "–" rows). All match §8.6.
- **Steam store API** [21]: GTA V Legacy 271590 "13 Apr, 2015" (matches §8.3 note); GTA V Enhanced 3240220 "Mar 4, 2025", age 17 (matches); Trilogy DE SA 1547000 "Jan 19, 2023" (matches §10.1 / §12.5); GTA IV CE 12210 "Mar 24, 2020" (extra data point, 14.3).
- **Cartridge codes vs gbhwdb** [25]: GTA GBC DMG-AOAE-0 / DMG-AOAP-0 and GTA 2 GBC CGB-BGAE-0 / CGB-BGTP-0 match §1.5 / §2.2.
- **Serial/version labels vs Redump/No-Intro DATs** [14]: SA PS2 (SLUS-20946 v1.03/v3.00/Rev 1, SLES-52541 v1.03/v2.01, SLES-52927 v1.00/v2.01, SLPM-65984/66788/55292), CW DS (YGXE/YGXP/YGXJ) and GTA Advance (BGTE/BGTP) match §5.2 / §6.

Total spot-checked: **≈150 values; 0 factual errors found** in dates, serials or versions. The errors in 14.3 are stale or inconsistent statements.

Additional consistency fixes made by the coordinator in the final pass: the GTA (1997) Japanese PC release date now shows both 27 Apr 1999 (GTA.wiki) and 23 Jan 1998 (GameFAQs) in §1, matching §13; Google Play `.de` package suffixes were confirmed to mean "Definitive" (not German) in §10; PC executable builds recognised by Plugin-SDK were added for GTA III (§3.4a) and Vice City (§4.3a).

## Appendix A. Preserved Retail Disc & Cartridge Dumps (Redump / No-Intro)

This appendix lists every Grand Theft Auto entry found in the Redump (optical discs) and No-Intro (cartridges, digital/PSN/XBLA packages, title updates) preservation databases, as mirrored in the libretro-database repository on GitHub (snapshot cloned 2026-10-24) [A1][A2][A3]. Each row is one verified dump of a distinct retail release, revision or digital package; the dump name carries the database's own region, language and version/revision labels (e.g. `(v1.03)`, `(Rev 1)`, `(EDC)`). Multiple serials on one row mean the same disc image was found under several catalogue numbers (reprints such as Platinum/Greatest Hits, or ANZ/regional SKUs). Size is the total of all tracks/files in bytes; CRC32 is of the first track/file. ✅ for all rows: these are checksum-verified dumps.
